THIS IS A DECISION, NOT A BACKLOG ITEM — it is not "we never got to it", and it has a REOPEN TRIGGER, in the same discipline as the exits closure.** **DEPLOYED off-market `488e7e3`** (`d31759f` §A ShadowTracker OFF · `488e7e3` §B M-A2 8s; crontab byte-IDENTICAL; service NOT restarted — `inactive`, `NRestarts=0`, start still Fri 24-Jul 08:15:27; both land Mon 27-Jul 08:15). **⭐ §C — THE DECISION: leave the tick feed dormant. Nothing subscribes; nothing ever has** (`live_feed.subscribe()` is never called in any boot path — the socket has **connected 32 times and carried 0 tokens, 0 ticks, ever**). **THE FOUR LEGS OF THE REASONING:** (1) **the 15:40 historical 1-minute fetch covers every ANALYTICAL need and carries TRUE DELTA volume** — the tick path under `MODE_LTP` carries **no** `volume_traded` at all, so wiring it would give strictly WORSE data; (2) **four of the five starved consumers are blocked INDEPENDENTLY anyway** — `smart_tgt` (never registered: `order_placer.py:949` sets the protocol unconditionally, `smart_tgt_state`=0 rows) · `StructureExitManager` (`structure_exit_enabled: false`, deliberate) · `BreakevenManager` (**never constructed** in `main.py`, whole history) · so a subscription alone would light up none of them; (3) **the fifth (`ShadowTracker`) is now DISABLED** (§A below); (4) ⭐ **EVERY feature the tick feed would serve is an EXIT feature, and the exits thread is CLOSED on measured numbers** (8 policies over 115 real trades with real costs: current −0.100R, best alternative −0.076R, worst −0.144R — **none profitable**). ⇒ **wiring it would cost real work to feed features that are themselves closed or dark.** **⭐⭐ THE REOPEN TRIGGER (a trigger, not a note — revisit ONLY if):** **(a) a LIVE SAFETY dependency on ticks is identified** — none exists today: the broker-side SL is independent of the feed, the tick-age watchdog and H-9 guard are the feed's own health checks (not position protection), and **⭐ FIX-029's consumer-thread death detector — the one genuine safety casualty — was un-gated on 25-Jul (B1) and no longer depends on ticks arriving**; **or (b) the ENTRY work materially changes and the exits are re-derived post-M-S4** (the same trigger the exits closure carries). **Any earlier reopen is a mistake.** ⚠️ **TWO HAZARDS CARRIED FORWARD so a future fix does not hit them: (i) SYNTHETIC-CANDLE STICKINESS** — one real tick gives a token `_history`, after which `_close_candles()` emits a carry-forward candle for it **every 60 s for the rest of the process, even after `unsubscribe()`** (no eviction but `gc_sweep`/`MAX_HISTORY`), each fanning out to every callback **including a `_persist_candle` DB insert** ⇒ subscribe-then-forget leaks a permanent 1/min write per token ever touched; **(ii) ⛔ THE MODE_FULL ORDERING TRAP — flipping to `MODE_FULL` would ACTIVATE M-D1's volume corruption** (`volume_traded` is CUMULATIVE and `candle_store.py:63` SUMS it per tick), which is latent **only** because MODE_LTP delivers nothing. **M-D1 FIRST, ALWAYS.** MODE_LTP is sufficient for every consumer (OHLC is built from LTP by construction, Audit 3.4). **⭐ §C5 — `_persist_candle` IS FULLY REDUNDANT:** wiring it would add a **second, worse-quality writer** to `analytics.candles`, a table the 15:40 historical fetch already fills correctly (275,129 rows, true delta volume) ⇒ that consumer should stay dark even if the feed is ever wired. **✅ §A SHADOWTRACKER OFF — DEPLOYED `d31759f`** (config-only, component intact; VM verified reading `enabled: false`). > **📌 THE TWO ANSWERS WORTH REMEMBERING:** **(1) the day's realized P&L SURVIVES A RESTART BY CONSTRUCTION** — `FIX-051` removed the in-memory `_daily_pnl`; `get_daily_realized_net_pnl` recomputes from `fm_ledger` on every read and **both** daily-loss halves read it that way (`fund_manager.py:1279` gate, `:1507` snapshot) ⇒ **a restart does NOT hand the system a fresh loss budget**, and the limit still enforces on the restored value. **(2) kill state survives via `KillSwitch.__init__` → `_load_state_from_store()` (`kill_switch.py:250`, KS3) — NOT via `clear_stale_state`,** which returns early when the in-memory `_state` is INACTIVE, exactly what a fresh process has. Same-day kills persist; prior-day auto-clear (Rama's 20-Jun headless decision). **⚠️ A `PENDING_FILL` reservation is deliberately NOT restored** — the DB cannot know whether the broker filled it, so capital returns to available and the startup reconcile adopts-or-fails it against the broker. Removing that subtraction would silently inflate live reservable capital on a warm restart; it is now **pinned structurally**.
> **📌 PERMANENT RULE (third in the series):** **LIVE vs LATENT decides stop-and-report** — judge a finding by **REACHABILITY**, not by whether it matches the trigger phrase. LIVE (can fire under current production config) ⇒ STOP. LATENT (cannot fire today, but the config holds the trigger or one wiring change arms it) ⇒ CONTINUE, document prominently, **pin it with a test that fails the moment it becomes reachable**, and escalate. Joins *CONFIGURED IS NOT COVERED* and *WIRED IS NOT REACHABLE*.

Report `docs/audit/q9_batch4_sizing_floors_caps_18jul2026.md`; memory `q9_batch4_sizing_reachability_18jul`. **TEST-ONLY — no production file, no behaviour change.**
> **📌📌 THREE PERMANENT RULES RECORDED HERE:**
> **(1) "WIRED IS NOT REACHABLE"** — the sibling of Q9's *configured is not covered*. A guard can be correctly implemented, correctly wired and bite-proven in a fixture **and still never bind under production config**, because an earlier guard always clamps first. A green wired test then says nothing about whether it protects real capital. **REACHABILITY is now a permanent field in the Q9 coverage matrix.** Concretely: concentration is the strict unique minimum on **298/298** sized trades (`qty_by_risk <= qty_by_concentration` in **0** rows), so `qty_by_risk`, `qty_by_capital`, `POSITION_VALUE_CAP`, `BELOW_MIN`, the lot-skew guard, lot rounding and the FLAT ceiling are all **dead by algebra or config**.
> **(2) A REGRESSION BASELINE TAKEN IN A BARE `git worktree` IS INFLATED — AND INFLATION IS THE MASKING DIRECTION.** `config/instruments.csv` (75,749 B) is **git-ignored**, so a fresh worktree lacks it → `main.py:1949` cannot build the InstrumentCache → **`main.py:2014` `assert instrument_cache is not None`** trips → **~30 boot-path `test_main.py` + preflight/T4 tests fail spuriously** (proven causally: the test fails in **isolation in 0.56s**, passes in the main tree, and copying that one file in makes it pass; full base re-run went **42F → 15F**). A genuine new failure can then coincidentally match one of those ~30 and be waved through as pre-existing. **⇒ PREFER `git checkout <base> -- <files>` IN THE MAIN TREE** (it preserves git-ignored runtime data), or seed the worktree with the ignored files before trusting its numbers. **`git stash` remains FORBIDDEN**, and base+mine must be captured in the **SAME TIME WINDOW**. *This retires the "~30-test flake band" as an uncharacterised observation.*
> **(3) A FAILURE-SET COMPARISON ACROSS TWO SHELLS IS CORRUPTED BY ENCODING, NOT CODE — AND THE ARTIFACT INFLATES THE DELTA.** PowerShell `Set-Content -Encoding utf8` writes a **UTF-8 BOM + CRLF**; bash `sort`/`comm` write **LF, no BOM**. Diff a PowerShell-written failure list against a bash-written one and **every line mismatches on invisible bytes** → `comm -23` reported **32 phantom "new" failures** (this batch's regression gate) that were byte-for-byte the SAME test IDs. **⭐ THE TELL: identical COUNTS and identical IDs but a non-empty `comm` delta ⇒ suspect encoding, NOT a regression** — do NOT investigate the phantoms as new failures; **re-extract BOTH sides in the SAME shell**, or normalise before comparing (strip BOM + `\r`: `sed 's/\r$//'` / `dos2unix`). Sibling of (2): an **environment artifact masquerading as a code difference**, and inflation is again the dangerous direction — imaginary new failures waved through, or a genuine one lost among the phantoms. *This is the exact trap the next PowerShell↔bash failure-set compare will hit.*
> **⚠️ THREE FINDINGS RECORDED, NOT FIXED (evidence for D1 — nothing acted on):** **(a)** `PerformanceAllocator` is **never instantiated** anywhere and `perf_weights` is **never passed** to `SignalProcessor` ⇒ `perf_weight` ≡ **1.0** (298/298 trades) ⇒ `dynamic_by_winrate` / `min_multiplier` / `max_multiplier` are **read by nothing on the sizing path**. **(b)** `capital/position_sizer.py:506`'s `min(tiered, raw_qty*2)` ceiling permits **2× the tightest clamp arm** (20% of capital against a 10% cap; the 40% value cap cannot catch it; the stored `binding_constraint` still reads `'concentration'`) — **LATENT, not live**, only because of (a); a test now **fails the moment the allocator is wired**. **(c)** `reports/daily_report.py:464` reports **3,098 "capital" rejections when the true count is 0** — all are CONCENTRATION, matched because the reason string embeds `capital_qty=`; `:549` further fragments that one class into **246** lines.

# ⚠️ READ THIS BEFORE TOUCHING ANYTHING ⚠️

This is the single authoritative path/ops reference. It complements (does not
replace) the design/runbook docs in `docs/` — see "Related Docs" at the bottom.
For a one-screen quick reference, see [`/PATHS.md`](../PATHS.md).

## V3 SHARED-ENGINE — Common Utilities substrate (Phase-0 map + Step-1 build, 11-Jul-2026)

Phase-0 gap analysis: `docs/v3/V3_PHASE0_INVESTIGATION_GAP_ANALYSIS.md` (Common Utils + 03.01–03.09, ADAPT/NEW vs `main`@`8116b74`). The V3 "Common Utilities" tier is a **LOGICAL grouping of EXISTING pure helpers (reused as-is, NOT moved/rewritten)** plus two genuinely-new pure helpers built in Step 1. No existing helper was relocated (avoids churn/regressions).

**Substrate = REUSE (do NOT rebuild or duplicate):**
| Helper | Path | What |
|---|---|---|
| Price/tick/SL/TGT/clamp math | `orders/price_math.py` | pure Decimal math (`round_to_tick`, `calc_sl_price`, `calc_tgt_price`, `marketable_limit_price`, `clamp_exit_into_band`) |
| Shared OHLCV candle type | `sr_detector/models.py::Candle` | the ONE candle value object (frozen; `ts,open,high,low,close,volume`; `from_kite`) — reused everywhere, no parallel type |
| Swing-pivot detection | `sr_detector/pivots.py::find_swing_pivots` | pure local-extreme swings |
| Live 1m candle builder | `data/candle_store.py::CandleStore` | stateful LTP→OHLC (not pure; has clock thread) |
| IST clock | `core/time_authority.py` | `now_ist`/`today_ist`/`ist_timezone` |
| Sessions/holidays | `core/market_windows.py::MarketWindows` | injected-config trading-day/session/EOD authority (layer-1, stdlib-only) |
| Logging | `core/logger.py` | JSON-lines logging facade |
| Config reader | `core/config_loader.py::load_all` | single-source `AppConfig` (extra="forbid") |

**NEW (Step 1 build — `core/candle_math.py`, a domain-agnostic layer-1 pure module):**
- `true_range_series(candles) -> list[float]` + `atr(candles, period, method="wilder"|"sma") -> float|None` — Wilder's smoothing (default) or SMA of true ranges; the degenerate first TR (no prev close) is excluded, so the data requirement is `period+1` candles → fewer returns **None** (never fabricated). `period` is a parameter (no baked-in default). Fills the Phase-0 gap: ATR previously only consumed pre-computed; ATR-based SL/TGT was unimplemented (FIXED_PCT fallback).
- `resample(base_candles, target_tf, session_bounds) -> ResampleResult` — base→higher timeframe (int minutes, or `"day"`). Open=first/High=max/Low=min/Close=last/Volume=sum; ts=bucket START; buckets anchored to each date's **session open via injected `session_bounds`** (never hardcodes 09:15; no cross-day merge; pre-open/after-hours excluded). Honest flags on `ResampleResult`: `empty_bucket_starts` (gaps, not fabricated) + `partial_bucket_starts`/`partial_last` (session-tail bucket, e.g. NSE 375-min day's 15-min tail on a 30m grid — emitted from data present, never padded).
- **Placement rationale (R2/R3):** put in `core/candle_math.py` (domain-agnostic, NOT in `price_math.py` [order math] or `sr_detector/` [S&R-specific]). `atr`/`true_range_series` reference no project module. `resample` is the only Candle constructor and imports the shared `Candle` via a **function-local** `from sr_detector.models import Candle` — mirroring the established `# local import avoids cycle` pattern in `core/market_windows.py` — so `core.candle_math` has **no import-time dependency on `sr_detector`** (verified: importing `core.candle_math` does not load `sr_detector`; layer inversion avoided).
- **Wiring: NONE yet.** Step 1 is build + unit-test only; nothing in the live trading/scoring/sizing/S&R path consumes these. Later modules (03.01 S&R, 03.02 Regime, 03.06 Sizing) wire them in and own the config defaults. **No existing file modified; no live behaviour change; paper==live by construction (pure, no mode branch).**
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/test_candle_math.py` (31 = 26 + 5 `session_vwap`: hand-computed TR/ATR Wilder+SMA + VWAP, insufficient→None, determinism, invalid-param raises; resample 1m→5m / 5m→30m OHLCV, session alignment, partial-tail flag, empty-bucket flag, no cross-day merge, pre/post-hours exclusion, daily, tz preservation, MarketWindows-derived bounds). sr_detector regression 64 pass; main suite collects 4448 clean. **BUILT-but-UNPUSHED** (Rama pushes off-market) — see UNPUSHED_PENDING_DEPLOY_LEDGER. memory `v3_step1_common_utils_11jul`. (Step 2 added `session_vwap`.)

## V3 SHARED-ENGINE — 03.01 S&R Detection ADAPT (Step 2, 11-Jul-2026, SHADOW, default-OFF)

ADAPT of the existing `sr_detector/` observer (no parallel module). Adds Layer-A VWAP/ORB anchors, confirms Layer-B 30m/1h structural swings with role labels, exposes `confidence_class` (default ANCHOR_ONLY), and a manual-marking level EXPORT. **Stays shadow/observer-only — gates NO capital.** All net-new behaviour is gated by ONE default-OFF flag so the live shadow row + the 64 existing sr_detector tests are byte-identical.

- **VWAP + ORB anchors (Task 1):** `sr_detector/zone_builder.py` — `intraday_anchors(fine_candles, on_date, session_open, session_close, orb_window_minutes)` = VWAP (via new `core.candle_math.session_vwap`) + ORB high/low (max/min over `[session_open, +orb_window)`); `build_anchor_payload` merges these with the EXISTING PDH/PDL/PDC (`prior_day_levels`) + `round_number_levels`. Session bounds are INJECTED from `MarketWindows` (main.py) — never hardcoded. Today-only fine fetch via new `OhlcFetcher.fetch_interval(symbol, interval, lookback_days)` (`fetch_timeframes` unchanged).
- **Structural TF policy (Task 2):** `sr_detector/models.py::TF_ROLE` = {30minute→PRIMARY, 60minute→MAJOR, day→DAILY, 15/5minute→MINOR/MICRO}; `zone_builder.build_swings_payload(scored_zones)` groups the existing confluence-scored swings by role. Structural swings already computed on the fetched 30m/60m (confirmed) — this labels them for validation.
- **confidence_class (Task 3):** `SRAnalysis.confidence_class` (additive field) = `CONF_ANCHOR_ONLY` (default) | `CONF_ANCHOR_PLUS_VALIDATED_SWINGS`. **Never flips to VALIDATED in this step** — the flip is a later, human-gated action after the manual-marking validation passes. The 03.03 R:R gate will use ANCHOR-only levels for live capital until then.
- **Level export (Task 4):** `scripts/sr_level_export.py` — READ-ONLY; dumps anchors + swings (with TF/role + confidence) per symbol×date to a flat CSV Rama compares against his manual chart marks. Pure core (`flatten_analysis`/`rows_to_csv`/`export_levels`, injected analyze fn) is unit-tested; `main()` wires a read-only market-data kite + detector (anchors on) per as-of date. `python scripts/sr_level_export.py --symbols RELIANCE,TCS --dates 2026-07-10`.
- **Config (`core/config_loader.py` + `system_config.yaml`):** `sr_detector.intraday_anchors_enabled` (default **false** — master gate for the anchor fetch/emission), `anchor_intraday_interval` (5minute, validated intraday-only), `anchor_lookback_days` (1), `orb_window_minutes` (15). `enabled` stays true (V1 shadow armed). Flip `intraday_anchors_enabled` true AFTER the export validation passes.
- **Persistence:** the anchor/swing/confidence_class payload folds into the EXISTING `confluence_evidence` JSON column (`insert_sr_detector_result` reads only `_SR_INSERT_COLS`, ignores extras) — **NO schema change**. When the gate is off, the row is byte-identical to before.
- **Wiring:** `sr_detector/detector.py::analyze` (gated enrichment + `_build_anchors`), `_write` (gated evidence fold-in); `build_sr_detector`/`SRDetector` gain optional `session_open`/`session_close`; main.py passes `market_windows.market_open/close`. `intended_entry` is the round-number/nearest reference.
- **R4 regression:** the 64 sr_detector tests PASS UNCHANGED; **clean-tree vs dirty-tree suspect-file failures are IDENTICAL** (proved via stash-diff) → zero new failures; the 41 full-suite failures are the pre-existing PC-env baseline (test_main/fix061/phase17, green on VM). Changed-area set 148 pass (sr_detector 64 + sr_v2 37 + candle_math 31 + sr_anchors 12 + sr_level_export 4). Paper==live (pure, no mode branch). **BUILT-but-UNPUSHED.** Tests `tests/unit/test_sr_anchors.py` (12) + `test_sr_level_export.py` (4). memory `v3_step2_sr_detection_11jul`.

## V3 SHARED-ENGINE — 03.02 Market Regime engine (Step 3, 11-Jul-2026, NEW, SHADOW, default-OFF)

Pure NEW package `regime/` (Phase-0 grep negative confirmed). Emits a THREE-AXIS index-level regime (direction / volatility / day-type), each with an ordinal confidence → preference multiplier, plus a positive-confirmation-only extreme flag. **SHADOW — gates NOTHING** (no order/score/size/kill-switch); fail-safe by construction (a broken regime NEVER halts the book). Reuses Common Utilities + the existing OHLC fetch; no duplicated ATR/candle/session/calendar/fetch logic.

- **Index data (TASK 0):** the NIFTY index is **NOT in the instrument cache** (`instruments.csv` has no index rows; token 256265 lives only in test fixtures). Reused the existing rate-limited fetch closure via new **`OhlcFetcher.fetch_by_token(token, interval, lookback_days)`** (skips the symbol→token cache) with the index token+symbol from **config** (`regime.index_token` 256265 / `index_symbol` "NIFTY 50"). NOT a new data path — the same `_make_sr_fetch_fn` closure. **⚠️ VM must verify live index historical-data access before flipping `regime.enabled`; fail-safe covers absence (→ UNKNOWN).**
- **New pure indicators (`core/candle_math.py`):** `ema_series`/`ema` (SMA-seeded), `adx` (Wilder, reuses `true_range_series`; needs ≥2·period+1 candles → None). Live with `atr`/`session_vwap` in the substrate (reused, not duplicated).
- **Axes (`regime/engine.py`):** DIRECTION (BULL/BEAR/SIDEWAYS) = price vs EMA50/EMA200 + EMA slope + HH/HL via `find_swing_pivots` + ADX, confidence from sub-signal agreement (≥4 & trending → HIGH). VOLATILITY (HIGH/NORMAL/LOW) = cur ATR / baseline TR ratio vs config bands. DAY-TYPE (TREND_DAY/RANGE_DAY/UNDETERMINED) = intraday range/ATR + ADX + one-directionality + position-in-range; **early session → UNDETERMINED LOW** (session_progress from MarketWindows). Confidence rises with session progress.
- **Ordinal confidence → multiplier (`regime/models.py`):** HIGH 1.0 · MEDIUM 0.5 · LOW 0.2 · TRANSITION 0.0 (`CONFIDENCE_MULTIPLIER`). `RegimeState.preference_multiplier` = the direction axis (headline); each axis carries its own. Ordinal FIRST (no faked % precision).
- **Extreme flag (TASK 5):** TRUE **only** on a CONFIRMED halt/circuit from the injected `exchange_status_fn` (positive confirmation). A **MISSING feed → FALSE + CRITICAL log** (absence ≠ halt). **Emitted + logged but NOT wired to stop trading** (03.03 consumes it later).
- **Fail-safe (TASK 6):** missing/insufficient index candles → `unknown_state` (status UNKNOWN, every axis neutral, multiplier 0 — the book trades normally, NOT a stop). Per-axis error → most-uncertain value at TRANSITION. Any exception in `compute()` → UNKNOWN + neutral (never raises).
- **Shadow runner (`regime/runner.py`):** `MarketRegimeShadowRunner` computes once per cycle during market hours, logs, and persists the latest to `data_store/regime/regime_state.json` (best-effort; for later prior_day_regime). Default-off.
- **Config:** `system_config.yaml regime:` block + `core/config_loader.py::RegimeConfig` (`enabled: false` default; index token/symbol, EMA/ADX/ATR periods, vol ratios, thresholds; validators: intraday interval + ema_fast<ema_slow). Registered on SystemConfig.
- **Wiring (`main.py`, default-OFF):** built + started only when `regime.enabled` (shares `_sr_fetch_fn`; own OhlcFetcher; `exchange_status_fn=None`); stopped in `_shutdown`. Inert by default → no live behaviour change.
- **R4 regression:** regime is NEW (no callers). Existing touches: `main.py` (dormant block + shutdown hook), `core/config_loader.py` (+RegimeConfig), `sr_detector/fetch.py` (+fetch_by_token, `fetch_timeframes`/`fetch_interval` unchanged), `core/candle_math.py` (+ema/adx), `config/system_config.yaml` (+regime block). **test_main clean-tree vs dirty-tree failures IDENTICAL (26==26, stash-diff) → zero new failures.** Spec tests `tests/unit/test_market_regime.py` (T1–T8 + fail-safe + determinism + runner, 13) + candle_math +9 (ema/adx). Paper==live (pure, injected collaborators, no mode branch). **BUILT-but-UNPUSHED.** memory `v3_step3_market_regime_11jul`.

Design doc: **`docs/v3/V3_STEP6_PORTFOLIO_ALLOCATOR_PLAN.md`** (P1-P10, for Web Claude + ChatGPT + Rama). **No code/config/behaviour changed; ledger untouched.** Plan for the biggest NEW build — replace **FCFS/concurrent admission** (dispatcher drains queue → 5-worker pool, each signal runs the whole pipeline independently, NO candidate set assembled/ranked; "Priority rank #1 of 1" `signal_processor.py:439` is a placeholder) with **batch-over-a-short-candle-window → rank by score → greedy admit within portfolio rules → atomic reserve via EXISTING primitives**. **Single seam** = the PREPARE→ADMIT boundary inside `_process_one` (`signal_processor.py`): PREPARE (pre-flight→strategy→screen→size, ends `:904`) stays 5-worker-concurrent; ADMIT (the `portfolio_lock` block `:919-960`: `_enforce_strategy_position_cap`→`risk.approve`→`fm.reserve`→RESERVED→place) is extracted so both the fused OFF path and the batch worker invoke it. **SITS ON** (reimplements none): `fm.reserve`(`fund_manager.py:473`), `portfolio_lock`(RLock `:382`), `count_live_reservations[_for_strategy]`(`:1424`/`:1440`), `risk.approve` 10 gates(`risk_engine.py:177`), `_enforce_strategy_position_cap`(`:589`). **`allocator_mode: off|shadow|enforce` default `off` = BYTE-IDENTICAL** (fused path; buffer never entered). **shadow** = non-blocking observer emits a scored-candidate copy at `:904`, computes the would-be ranked set vs live FCFS + logs REGRET (never reserves/places; live timing+orders unchanged; counterfactual is directional-approximate — flagged). **enforce** = single admission worker ranks `(score DESC, tier, triggered_at, signal_id)` + greedy-admits the batch under ONE portfolio_lock (counts/capital auto reservation-aware via the primitives; LOG-ONLY: the verdict NEVER rejects/delays/alters a live order. regime read O(1) from `runner.latest` AS OF signal time (None when regime OFF → the chain runs correctly with regime off). Soak: `scripts/v3_shadow_soak_report.py --v3` = the **G-KALYAN join** (would-be R:R verdict × REAL trade P&L → did the S&R R:R gate reject our winners or our losers?). **Acceptance:** G-OFF byte-identical (test_signal_processor 77 unchanged + test_main clean-vs-dirty 4==4 stash-diff → zero-new); G-NO-INTERFERENCE (a shadow chain that RAISES on observe → live outcome UNCHANGED + hook fires, test); G-HOTPATH (no hot-path fetch; O(1) regime; fire-and-forget guarded); G-CONFLUENCE (grouped pair = one weight, test); anti-lookahead V2 (a future candle does NOT alter the verdict, test); RAMCOIND 264 + full-suite 4516 collect clean. Tests `tests/unit/test_v3_chain.py` (20). paper==live. **BUILT-but-UNPUSHED; STOPPED AT OFF — no live behaviour changed.** memory `v3_step10a_chain_impl_12jul`. NEXT = Web Claude + ChatGPT review → (Rama/off-market) push → shadow soak (the Kalyan measurement) → Step 10b (PB-01 + playbook gates + Playbook score layer + overnight watchlist).

## GUI Project (Frontend) — Phase G0 investigation COMPLETE (2026-07-02)
> Rama's pending decision #8 (build a Frontend GUI) is moving. **Phase G0 = backend investigation only** (zero code changes) is DONE. **Report:** [`docs/gui_project/G0_BACKEND_INVESTIGATION_REPORT.md`](gui_project/G0_BACKEND_INVESTIGATION_REPORT.md) — 8 sections, every claim cited `file:line`, produced from 5 parallel read-only investigations + direct verification of the control (§3) & security (§6) sections.
> **Locked v1 decisions (Rama, 02-Jul):** same Oracle VM · browser-from-anywhere, HTTPS-only, single-user, auth-required · runs while PC off · desktop-first + mobile-responsive · control depth = view + safe controls (pause/resume, enable/disable, kill pending orders, manual square-off, emergency halt, restart) · ~20 view modules.
> **5 design-critical findings (facts, not design):** (1) TWO existing Flask+Waitress HTTP servers as daemon-threads inside `main.py` (webhook :5000 authed · healthcheck/metrics **:8080 UNAUTHENTICATED**, exposes P&L/capital/kill-switch) — both `0.0.0.0`, plain HTTP, **NO TLS/reverse proxy**; up only while the trader runs. (2) **Control plane barely exists** — only clear-kill-switch (CLI) + `systemctl restart` are operator surfaces; pause/enable-disable/cancel-orders/square-off/trigger-halt have **NO programmatic entry point**, strategy/config edits need **restart (no hot reload)**, **no inbound Telegram**; all existing paths are paper==live. (3) **No cross-process push** (EventBus is in-process only) → GUI polls SQLite (WAL + 30 s busy_timeout ⇒ read-only reader safe) or the in-process `/metrics`; **live LTP not persisted** → needs its own broker session. (4) DB = 43 main + 3 analytics tables; **no `positions`/`holdings` table** (status-filtered `trades` / CNC via `gtt_state`); data exists for nearly all view modules. (5) The **18:00–08:00 time-lock is copy-gate (scp/sftp/rsync) only — it does NOT block nighttime browser access**; `security_monitor`'s checks never inspect listening ports (9 security checks + a 10th, added 26-Jul, that is deliberately NOT a security check — see `security-watcher.service` below); a GUI writing under `config/`/`​.env` trips auditd, writes to `data_store/logs/reports` do not.
> **[REQUIRES LIVE VM]** (not run from PC this pass): `systemctl is-active`, `ss -tlnp`, live fail2ban/auditctl rules, RAM/CPU/disk headroom (documented floor 2 vCPU / 2 GB / 20 GB; psutil absent ⇒ CPU/RAM not persisted). memory `gui_g0_backend_investigation_02jul`.

### ⚠️ ARCHITECTURAL RULE — SCHEMA MIGRATIONS RUN ON DB-OPEN, NOT "AT BOOT" (14-Jul-2026)

⚠️ **SCHEMA VERSION SOURCE — ROLLBACK TRAP (24-Jul-2026):** the schema version lives in **`schema_meta`** (a `(key, value)` table): `SELECT value FROM schema_meta WHERE key='schema_version'` → **`44`**. **`PRAGMA user_version` is `0`** and is NOT the schema version — an operator doing a rollback who reads `user_version` (the natural reflex) will see `0` and wrongly conclude the DB is unversioned / version zero. **Read `schema_meta`, never the pragma.** (Verified live 24-Jul: `schema_meta.value='44'`, `PRAGMA user_version=0`.)
`StateStore.__init__` → `_initialize_schema()` reads the on-disk `schema_version` and, if it is **older** than the code's `EXPECTED_SCHEMA_VERSION`, runs `run_migrations()` (which **rebuilds** affected tables) then `executescript(schema.sql)`. **This happens whenever ANY process constructs a `StateStore` against the live DB with newer code** — a cron, a monitor, a report, or a research script — **whichever opens it first after a schema-changing push.** The 08:15 `main.py` boot is only the *first opener on a normal day*, NOT the migration mechanism.
- **Proof (13-Jul):** v42→v43 migrated at **19:30** (`cron_watchdog`); v43→v44 at **23:41** (`forward_shadow_record`). **Neither fired at boot.** Both were additive + off-market → harmless by luck of timing, not design.
- **RISK:** a schema pushed such that a **during-market** StateStore-opener runs first → migration (a live-table rebuild) runs **09:00–15:30 with `main.py` running on the DB**. The `cron_officer` cron opens the live DB at **09:20 + hourly** = the realistic mid-session trigger. The off-market-push convention is the *only* thing preventing this today; it is not enforced.
- **NOT triggers (by mechanism, not intent):** the GUI (`?mode=ro` — a ro conn can't `executescript`), `capture_metrics_baseline` (`*/5 09-15`, raw `db_connect`, no StateStore), `db_backup` (`sqlite3 .backup` CLI).
- **COROLLARY:** a "read-only verification" against the LIVE DB with newer code is **NOT read-only** — opening the StateStore migrates it. Always verify with `--db <copy>`.
- **Full inventory + a PROPOSED (not implemented) migration guard** (market-hours refusal / `--allow-migrate` / deploy preflight): `docs/audit/migration_on_open_rule_14jul2026.md`. Every future schema deploy MUST identify which process realistically opens the DB first and reason about WHEN. **Guard = PROPOSAL, PAUSED for review.**

### ⚠️ ENGINEERING RULE — A TEST HARNESS MUST NEVER HOLD A WRITABLE HANDLE ON A LIVE DB (18-Jul-2026)
**A destructive / crash-test harness must NEVER hold a writable handle on a live database. ALL writable DB access goes through ONE guard that fails CLOSED — no override flag, no environment escape hatch. Resetting a live system is an OPERATOR tool in `scripts/` with a backup + confirmation gate, NEVER part of a test harness.**
- **Why it exists:** `tests/crash_test/ct_utils.py` hardcoded the live DB and `get_db_connection()` defaulted to **writable**; `reports/crash_test/cleanup_log.jsonl` records real live writes on **05-Jun and 07-Jun 2026** ("Kill switch cleared (1 rows updated)", "Released 37 orphan reservations", a `hard_cleanup` that cancelled trades/orders and reset capital). Five further modules bypassed the guard entirely with raw `sqlite3.connect()` on a hardcoded live path.
- **The guard:** `ct_utils.assert_not_live_db()` → raises `LiveDatabaseRefused`. Compares canonical `realpath` **plus `os.path.samefile()`** (the only thing that catches a **hardlink**, which `realpath` cannot resolve) and refuses the `-wal`/`-shm` sidecars. Proven against absolute/relative/`..`/symlink/hardlink/env-override routes. **Read-only live access stays allowed** (`get_db_connection(readonly=True)`, `mode=ro`) — that is the harness's legitimate diagnostic use.
- **Enforced automatically, not by memory** — `tests/crash_test/test_ct_guard_invariant.py` (AST scan of every harness module): **(A)** no raw `sqlite3.connect()` outside `ct_utils.py`; **(B)** no live-DB path literal outside `ct_utils.py` (*you cannot open what you may not name*); **(C)** `StateStore(db_path=…)` never receives `LIVE_DB_PATH`. **Proven to bite:** a planted bypass makes A+B fail; removing it restores green. Allow-list is 3 files, each justified in-line.
- **Corollary (composes with the migration-on-open rule above):** a harness passing the live path to `StateStore` is doubly dangerous — it opens **writable** *and* can migrate the schema on open.
- Detail: `docs/audit/ct_harness_safety_18jul2026.md` · `docs/audit/ct_guard_invariant_and_secwatcher_18jul2026.md`. **⚠️ OPEN (Rama):** `cleanup.py --live` now REFUSES — does live-reset return as a `scripts/` operator tool with backup + confirmation?

  added the slippage-intelligence layer: order_execution_log + trade_slippage_log + market_execution_context;

  slippage tolerance override hierarchy**). trades, orders, signals, fm_ledger, etc.

- `data_store/candles/` — candle artifacts.
- `data_store/critical_alert_*.flag` — CRITICAL sentinels written by `alerts/critical.py`,
  consumed by `alert-watcher.service` (VM) → renamed to `.delivered`. **Local-only/gitignored**
  (`data_store/` is ignored). **Retention (23-Jun):** the `sentinel_retention` cron (02:05 daily)
  deletes `critical_alert_*.delivered` older than 7 days and **NEVER** touches `*.flag` (pending) —
  email is the authoritative record, so there is no archive. The PC dev tree has no watcher, so test
  runs that write real sentinels accumulate `.flag` there; **root-caused 23-Jun** by pointing
  `test_fix132_email_fallback.py`'s `sentinel_dir` at a tmp dir (was `"data_store"`). VM stays clean
  (0 `.flag`, watcher healthy); PC's 42 test flags removed 23-Jun (445 earlier on 19-Jun).

## PC Paths  (developer machine, Windows)

### Local Project
`D:\Projects\trading-system`  (this git repo; mirrors the VM working tree).

### SCP Deployment Map
**There is no SCP.** Deployment is **git push**:
```
PC:  git push origin main            # origin = vm = trading-vm:~/trading-system.git
VM:  bare repo post-receive hook  →  git checkout -f  →  /home/ubuntu/systems/trading-system
     (service is NOT restarted automatically — restart manually to activate code)
```
- Git remotes (PC): `origin` **and** `vm` both point to `trading-vm:~/trading-system.git` (duplicate; see Issues).
- SSH host alias `trading-vm` (key `trading_vm_secure`, passwordless).

### SATS — Static Analysis (PC-only, manual; never deploys)
`sats/` is **git-ignored** (the scanners never reach the VM). Two pre-installed isolated
venvs hold the tools; run the scan `.bat`s **on demand** — no hooks, no automation:
| What | Path |
|---|---|
| SATS root | `D:\Projects\trading-system\sats\` |
| Bandit exe | `sats\bandit-env\Scripts\bandit.exe` (1.9.4) |
| Semgrep exe | `sats\semgrep-env\Scripts\semgrep.exe` (1.167.0) |
| Scan scripts | `sats\scripts\scan_bandit.bat`, `sats\scripts\scan_semgrep.bat` |
| Reports | `sats\reports\{bandit,semgrep}_<yyyyMMdd_HHmmss>.txt` |
| Semgrep baseline | `sats\semgrep_baseline.txt` (pinned commit; only findings NEW since it are reported) |

- Both scan the repo root (`-r` / target = `D:\Projects\trading-system`), **exclude `venv,sats,.git`**,
  and write a timestamped txt report + echo it to the console (`chcp 65001`, locale-independent
  PowerShell timestamp, auto-create `reports\`, tool run ONCE via `-o`/`--output` then `type`).
- Semgrep rulesets `p/python` + `p/security-audit` (login-free; first run downloads, cached after).
- Bandit reports all severities; add `-ll` to filter to medium+ if noisy.
- Bandit `-x` uses **absolute** paths — `bandit/core/manager.py` matches each token both as an
  fnmatch glob **and** as a path substring.
- **Windows UTF-8:** both `.bat`s set `PYTHONUTF8=1` — without it Semgrep/Bandit **crash** writing the
  report (`UnicodeEncodeError`); `chcp 65001` fixes only the console, not Python's cp1252 file writes.
- **Semgrep baseline:** if `sats\semgrep_baseline.txt` exists, `scan_semgrep.bat` adds
  `--baseline-commit <hash>` (and runs from the repo root for git) so only findings **NEW** since that
  commit are shown; delete the file for a full scan. Pinned at `65439ff` (2026-06-22 SATS triage).
- ⚠️ **Do not** modify / activate / reinstall the two venvs.

## Cron Jobs  (live `crontab -l` == `deploy/cron/trading-system.cron` == `generate(registry)` — **44 command-lines as of 30-Jun** (was 43 @29-Jun; +`sr_detector_backfill` 15:58; prior +`reconstruct_excursions` 15:50 +`control_tower` 17:05; regenerate the sha after a registry change); **source of truth: `config/cron_registry.yaml`** — TASK #3 + 23-Jun framework. The table below is an illustrative summary — the canonical/registry are authoritative.)
All market jobs run `cd … && set -a && . ./.env && set +a && PYTHONPATH=. venv/bin/python <script> >> logs/<log>`.
To change cron: edit `config/cron_registry.yaml` → `scripts/generate_crontab.py --generate --out deploy/cron/trading-system.cron` → commit + push (the `post-receive` hook **auto-installs** on the VM; or manually `crontab deploy/cron/trading-system.cron`). `--gate` proves zero-drops (+ only `sentinel_retention`); `--selftest` proves byte round-trip.

> ⚠️ **FIX-189 (2026-06-19) — cron MUST run under bash.** cron's default `/bin/sh`
> is **dash**, whose `.` (source) builtin will NOT load a relative path without a
> slash → a bare `. .env` fails with `sh: .: .env: not found` and **every job dies
> before Python** (no token refresh, no heartbeats, no per-cron logs). The
> canonical file therefore carries TWO guards: a leading **`SHELL=/bin/bash`** line
> AND **`. ./.env`** (the `./` makes even dash source from CWD). Keep both on any
> regeneration. `tests/unit/test_cron_registry.py::TestFix189CronShell` enforces it.

> ⚠️ **ENV-EXPORT FIX (2026-06-19) — source MUST be `set -a && . ./.env && set +a`.**
> `.env` uses bare `VAR=value` (no `export`), so a plain `. ./.env` sets shell
> variables the child `python` does **NOT** inherit → every cron job ran WITHOUT
> its `.env` secrets (`ZERODHA_API_KEY_LFL836`, `TELEGRAM_*`).
> FIX-189 made jobs *run*; this made them get their secrets (the 19-Jun 15:45
> reconcile_positions broker-creds + "Telegram env not set" were both this).
> `set -a` (allexport) exports everything sourced. Keep the wrapper on every
> Python job line. `tests/unit/test_cron_registry.py::TestCronEnvExport` enforces it.

| Time (IST) | Days | Script | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| 00:00 | daily | `find logs -mtime +30 -delete` | log cleanup |
| 01:00 | daily | sqlite3 `.backup` trading_system.db | nightly DB backup |
| 01:05 | daily | sqlite3 `.backup` analytics.db | analytics DB backup (TASK #3) |
| 02:00 | daily | `backup_retention.py --apply` | **backup retention** (T2: category-aware keep-N — pre_*=20 / trading_system-*=14 / analytics-*=14; dry-run default, --apply in cron; replaces the old daily-only `find -mtime +7`; monitored+heartbeat) |

## ⚠️ MANDATORY — Broker-Truth Verification BEFORE Every Manual Position Intervention (16-Jul-2026)

**NEVER flatten / square-off / hedge from DB state alone. The DB status LAGS broker truth.**
On 16-Jul the DB read a position (ACI) as OPEN for minutes *after* its stop-loss had already
filled at the broker; a DB-driven "flatten the open position" would have SOLD a share we no
longer held → **a naked short**. Zerodha *regular* orders also have **no broker-side OCO** (the
engine enforces software-OCO for `LIMIT_TRIPLE`), so any manual action **races the exit-manager**
unless the engine is stopped first. Every manual intervention on positions MUST follow, in order:

1. **STOP the trading engine** (`sudo systemctl stop trading-system.service`, or a SOFT_KILL then
   restart — see below). Do not skip: a running engine will race your manual order.
2. **VERIFY the engine is fully stopped** (`systemctl is-active trading-system` → `inactive`; no
   `main.py` in `ps`). A half-stopped engine still manages exits.
3. **READ BROKER positions** — `kite.positions()` / the Kite web terminal is the **source of
   truth**, NOT `trades`/`positions` in the DB.
4. **COMPARE broker vs DB** and resolve every mismatch. If they disagree, **the broker wins.**
   (Per symbol: buys == sells ⇒ flat, regardless of what the DB row says.)
5. **FLATTEN ONLY broker-open positions** (net qty ≠ 0 at the broker). Never place an order for a
   position that is flat at the broker even if the DB shows it open.
6. **VERIFY zero positions** at the broker after flattening (`kite.positions()` net == 0).
7. **VERIFY zero pending orders** at the broker (`kite.orders()` — cancel any resting SL/TGT legs;
   regular orders have no OCO, so an orphan leg can fill later).
8. **RESTART only when intended** (engine boot re-reads the kill switch; an active SOFT_KILL boots
   to `startup_scenario=HALT` → exit-4, designed no-restart — see the kill-switch note below).

**Corollary — no live halt/flatten API:** the KillSwitch is loaded **at boot only** (in-memory), so
an intra-session stop needs **stop → set-kill → start → HALT (exit-4)**; there is no running-process
"flatten now" endpoint (recorded as a future architecture decision, not built). See `deploy/resume.sh`
/ `scripts/clear_kill_switch.py` to resume. This checklist is the **"broker-truth before every manual
intervention"** rule — memory `alertwatcher_loop_fix_16jul`.

## Known Duplicates / Issues  (audit 2026-06-18; cleanup applied same day)
1. ~~Live crontab ≠ `deploy/cron/trading-system.cron`~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-06-18 (TASK #3)**: the
   registry `config/cron_registry.yaml` is now the source of truth; the canonical file was regenerated
   and **installed** (`crontab deploy/cron/trading-system.cron`) — live == file (33 lines). The
   previously-missing analytics.db backup (01:05) + `db_retention` (02:30) were added; `auto_refresh_token`
   moved 08:00→08:15. Old crontab backed up to `data_store/crontab_backups/`.
2. **[PARTIAL] `trading-system.service` — emergency-kill recovery + headless** — crash-loop FIXED
   (`RestartPreventExitStatus=3 4`). FIX-188 (18-Jun) additionally stopped **token-watcher** from
   hammer-restarting an exit-4 HALT every 30s (now exit-code aware: same-day HALT → back off + 1
   alert/day; prior-day HALT → one clean start so it auto-recovers) and broadened `/health` (token +
   kill_switch, 200/503). The emergency SOFT_KILL was cleared (operator `--resume`) and the service
   **handed off to systemd — now `active`, `NRestarts=0`**. Still OPEN (EXTERNAL): the Kite dev-console
   **IP allowlist** for the VM IP (place_order 403 root cause; see `kite_ip_allowlist_dependency`,
   FIX-185) — required for live order placement. **Resume cleanly with `deploy/resume.sh`** (stop →
   `scripts/clear_kill_switch.py` clears the kill in the DB without the instance lock → start under
   systemd) — FIX-188b; do NOT use a standalone `main.py --resume` (it competes for instance-lock port 5001).
   **X5 (17-Jul):** the lock is now OS-owned (`fcntl.flock` on the lock file) as well as the port, so a
   standalone `main.py` is refused by BOTH layers — the guidance above is unchanged, only harder to defeat.
   `clear_kill_switch.py` takes no instance lock, so `deploy/resume.sh` is unaffected. The lock file
   (`/tmp/trading-system.lock`) is deliberately NEVER unlinked: the lock lives on the inode via an open fd,
   and unlinking a path a live instance holds would let the next start lock a fresh inode and run alongside
   it. Its PID is informational — a stale/recycled PID no longer blocks a boot (that was the real X5 bug).
3. ~~`alert-watcher.service` SMTP delivery~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-06-18 (verified)**: valid Gmail App

- 🧪⚠️ **`tests/unit/test_main.py` leaked a MagicMock into `main._log` — FIXED 16-Jul (`f87e587`, test-only).**
  `main._main_locked` declares `global _log` (`main.py:1538`) and rebinds it (`_log = get_logger("main")`,
  `:1626`); test_main patches `main.get_logger`→MagicMock and the patch restores `get_logger` but **NOT
  `_log`** ⇒ after test_main ran, `main._log` stayed a MagicMock for the whole session and **ANY later test
  asserting on main's logging was SILENTLY VACUOUS** (passing while testing nothing — that is how M-C8's
  test_d3 was caught). Fix = an **autouse module fixture** saving/restoring `main._log` (autouse because there
  are **4** separate `patch.multiple("main", ...)` sites); guard `test_zz_main_log_is_not_left_as_a_mock` is
  deliberately **LAST in the file** — pytest runs tests in definition order, and last is the only position
  where an entry-invariant assertion means anything.

  `StateStore.count_active_positions()` counts ONLY `OPEN`/`PARTIAL`/`PENDING_FILL` — `EXITING` is BLIND

- 🧨✅ **M-C8 FIXED — the hard_kill flatten now runs on a WORKER, not the caller's thread (16-Jul,
  MERGED into the M-C cluster; `capital/kill_switch.py` + `main.py`; report
  `docs/audit/mc8_async_hardkill_16jul2026.md`, memory `mc8_async_hardkill_16jul`).**
  **`hard_kill` FIRES-AND-RETURNS on the adapter path** (production — `main.py:1983` always `set_adapter`);
  the kill STATE is still tripped synchronously first (`is_active` blocks orders immediately — that did NOT
  go async). The **LEGACY `cancel_fn` path stays SYNCHRONOUS** and `_exit_all_trades_indestructible` stays a
  sync internal method — **that split is LOAD-BEARING** (it is what keeps the 86 pre-existing kill-switch
  tests passing untouched). Do not "tidy" it. **⚠️ CORRECTION: the design's claim "no existing test calls
  `hard_kill()` with an adapter set / no test rewritten" was FALSE** —
  `test_p0_live_day1_fixes::TestBugC_KillSwitchExit` did exactly that and broke; those 3 now call
  `_exit_all_trades_indestructible()` directly like their siblings (assertions verbatim). **Only the FULL
  suite caught it.** A test that drives `hard_kill()` with an adapter gets a DISPATCH, not a result:
  `drain_flatten()` first, then assert.
  **Flatten state machine `IDLE→RUNNING→DRAINING→COMPLETE`** under a **DEDICATED lock, never the KS4 RLock**
  (reusing it would re-create M-C4 in a worse place: a 2h join under the lock that gates `is_active`);
  `COMPLETE` is set in a `finally` — a worker dying at RUNNING would make `is_flatten_in_progress()` answer
  True forever and hang the eod-self-exit gate all night. **SINGLE-FLIGHT on the adapter path** (a repeat/
  cross-thread `hard_kill` no-ops; the running loop already re-derives from broker truth every retry) —
  **KS6's "re-runs cancellation" is now PATH-SPECIFIC: preserved for legacy, single-flight for the adapter.**
  **⚠️ THE EXITING-BLIND RACE IS NOW CLOSED:** `main._eod_self_exit_due` gates on
  `KillSwitch.is_flatten_in_progress` (NOT `count_active_positions`), fail-safe (gate raises → stay up), and
  `_shutdown` drains the non-daemon worker **BEFORE any teardown** (the worker writes through `store`, and
  `store.close()` is at the bottom). **Shutdown split:** internal eod-exit waits (bounded by the flatten's own
  2h deadline); **external SIGTERM gets a bounded 15s grace** (`_FLATTEN_DRAIN_GRACE_SEC`) because
  `trading-system.service` sets `TimeoutStopSec=30` and systemd SIGKILLs regardless → CRITICAL "positions may
  remain open" on overrun. **DB writes are CONDITIONAL** (no global lock; broker truth stays authoritative):
  `trades→EXITING` conditioned on the live set, `orders→CANCELLED` on NOT-terminal, both sharing one module
  constant with the SELECTs that feed them. **📌 Two schema facts found while proving the tests RED-on-old:
  (1) `schema.sql:278 trg_trades_terminal_status_guard` ALREADY ABORTs transitions out of CLOSED/
  CLOSED_MANUAL/FAILED/CANCELLED/REJECTED* — so the terminal trades case was never exposed; the conditional's
  real gain there is `UNKNOWN_IN_FLIGHT` (the A-2 "don't know if it filled" state, NOT terminal, NOT guarded)
  plus turning a trigger-ABORT-logged-as-CRITICAL into a quiet no-op. (2) There is NO trigger on `orders` at
  all ⇒ the `CANCELLED`-over-`COMPLETE` clobber (a FILLED SL recorded as cancelled) was ENTIRELY unguarded —
  the conditional is the only thing preventing it.** Also hardened in self-review: a **thread-start failure
  runs the flatten INLINE** rather than wedging the state at RUNNING or dropping the flatten (an unflattened
  book beats a blocked caller), and **`drain_flatten` keys off the STATE not the thread handle** so an inline
  flatten cannot read as "nothing in flight". FIX-180/181/190/H-4/H-5 preserved by construction (the worker
  runs the same method); **27 new tests (9 proven RED-on-old by reverting each behaviour)**, 86 pre-existing
  kill-switch tests unchanged, `test_main` zero-new. **Deploys OFF-MARKET with the M-C cluster — emergency
  path ⇒ fresh combined regression + a sandbox hard_kill drill first.**

## Circuit-band placeability gate (NOCIL fix, 23-Jun-2026)
**Invariant:** a resting protective exit (SL stop / TGT limit) recalc'd from the fill must land on
the **correct side of the entry fill** *and* inside the day's circuit band — never a wrong-side,
instantly-marketable order. (NOCIL 22-Jun: a LONG TGT recalc'd to 197.12 was clamped DOWN to
`upper×0.98 = 187.00`, **below** the 189.78 fill → instant SELL, 2.35 s scratch mislabeled TGT_HIT.)

- **Primitive (single chokepoint):** `orders/price_math.py` → `clamp_exit_into_band(price, *, leg,
  direction, entry_fill, upper_circuit, lower_circuit, …) → ClampResult(price, was_clamped,
  placeable, reason)`. Frozen result; pure (no I/O). Replaces the old side-agnostic
  `clamp_to_circuit_band`. Margin = `DEFAULT_CIRCUIT_MARGIN_PCT` (0.02) — the **single source** shared
  with the pre-fill rule. Called from **exactly 3 sites, all in `orders/order_protocol_limit.py`**
  (SL + TGT in `place_exits`, TGT in `place_tgt_only`); a no-bypass test enforces this.
- **Leg asymmetry (caller branches on `placeable=False`):** **TGT** unplaceable → hold (SL-only;
  `tgt_placed=False` → FIX-190 Bug-C → `mark_needs_tgt_retry` → TGTRetryManager). **SL** unplaceable →
  position cannot be stopped → `raise SLUnplaceableError` (`core/exceptions.py`) → order_placer's
  existing unprotected-position escalation (`_emergency_market_exit` + `_fire_hard_kill_for_unprotected_position`).
  `entry_fill` (= settled `avg_fill_price`) is threaded fill→`place_deferred_exits`→`place_exits`.
- **HARD_KILL rationale (updated 2026-07-22, behaviour unchanged):** the
  `_fire_hard_kill_for_unprotected_position` escalation above no longer protects a *naked* position —
  since **FIX-148 (03-Jun)** the emergency flatten runs FIRST, so at kill time the position is normally
  already flat. The kill is **retained (Decision 7, Rama, 22-Jul)** on a different rationale — an
  **anomaly circuit-breaker**: an exit-placement rejection ⇒ halt the trading day (every live firing
  15/16/19-Jun was a multi-position storm), NOT naked-position protection (FIX-148 + the reverse-aware
  kill-flatten sweep already handle that). Contract: **OP-NS5** (`orders/order_placer.py` header).
  Forensics: `docs/audit/exit_rejection_hard_kill_forensics_22jul2026.md`.
- **Pre-fill reject (framing-b, both legs):** `screening/secondary_screener.py` rejects an entry that
  sits at/beyond the exit-clamp ceiling (`entry ≥ upper×(1−m)` LONG / `≤ lower×(1+m)` SHORT, and the
  symmetric SL-side) → `REJECTED_CIRCUIT_PROXIMITY`. Fast-disable lever:
  `entry_gate.circuit_proximity_reject_enabled` (default **true**). The post-fill gate is **not** flagged.
- **Documented un-gated exceptions (never clamp → no wrong-side risk; test-enforced):** CO-TGT
  (`order_protocol_co.py` — broker-managed CO SL backstops it) and the G5b recovery SL
  (`order_reconciler.py` — LTP-guarded). Both fail loud (broker reject), never a silent wrong-side fill.
- **Parity:** all shared entry/exit code, no mode branch → Paper + Live together. **No schema change.**

## MIS Learned Blocklist (source-free, 30-Jun) — NO_FILL Mechanism B fix
Fixes the broker MIS-block no-fills (Zerodha refuses MIS/intraday on non-MIS-list
stocks; `force_intraday_only` can't fall back to CNC → ~19 trades/9 symbols wasted,
0 fills ever). **Step 0 proved NO clean MIS source exists** (no API; RMS-dynamic
intraday; fragile/inconsistent Google Sheets) → so **NO daily list, NO `mis_tradable`
table, NO cron, NO schema, NO external source.** Instead: observe the broker's own 400.

| What | Location |
|---|---|
| Store + detector | `core/mis_blocklist.py` — `is_mis_block_rejection(exc)` (matches "mis orders are currently blocked") + `MisLearnedBlocklist` (persisted JSON `data_store/mis_blocklist.json` `{SYMBOL: last_blocked_date}`; thread-safe, atomic write, restart-safe, corrupt→fail-open) |
| RECORD (always-on) | `orders/order_placer.py::_handle_placement_failure` appends ONE guarded `record_block(symbol)` on a MIS-block 400; **every existing step unchanged**. Flag-independent so the list warms up while dormant |
| RE-TEST TTL | blocked iff in store AND `(today − last) < ttl_days` (default 5 calendar days). Expiry → allowed to re-test; fresh 400 refreshes; a trade self-corrects. Avoids permanent false-drop |
| DROP (flag-gated) | `screening/secondary_screener.py::screen()` step-0 hook → `REJECTED_NOT_MIS_TRADABLE` (signals.status GLOB 'REJECTED*' — no schema). Only when resolved product == MIS (future-proof via injected `resolve_product`). Logs every would-drop |
| Flag | `config/system_config.yaml` `mis_filter` (`core/config_loader.py` MisFilterConfig): `enabled:false` (default → DORMANT/byte-identical), `shadow:true` (log-only when enabled), `ttl_days:5`. Rollout OFF→enabled+shadow→shadow:false |
| Wiring / tests | `main.py` ONE shared instance → OrderPlacer (record) + SecondaryScreener (read). `tests/unit/test_mis_blocklist.py` (22). Parity: recording fires only on the live 400 (paper never 400s) |

> STAGED on branch `mis-tradability-filter-30jun`; NOT pushed. Daily-list path investigated + rejected in Step 0 (no clean source). Detail: memory `mis_learned_blocklist_30jun`.

## Control Tower (`ops/control_tower/`) — VM Operations monitoring (Phase 1, 29-Jun)
A READ-ONLY aggregator that reads the EXISTING monitors and adds data-freshness +
disk/backup checks. It NEVER blocks or acts on trading.

- **Daily run:** `ops/control_tower/runner.py` at **17:05 Mon-Fri** (cron job
  `control_tower`; supersedes the standalone size-logger). Order: size-logger
  (today's trend SIZE cols FIRST) → aggregator (detection → findings lifecycle →
  health → status roll-up → reporter). **Skips non-trading days** with a SKIPPED
  heartbeat (`--force` runs anyway, e.g. a preview). Records
  `cron_heartbeat("control_tower")`; `check_cron_drift` (18:00) is the external
  monitor (the runner is `self_job`-aware so it never flags its own in-flight run).
- **Adapters (4, read-only):** security `data_store/security/last_run.json`
  (written by `security_monitor`) · cron heartbeats + `cron_marks/*.done` · the
  Config Auditor (`audit_app_config`) · `excursion_reconstruction_runs`.
- **Freshness** (`freshness.py`): per stage candles(15:40)/signals/orders/
  executions/reconstruction(15:50)/eod(16:00) → OK | OVERDUE (>15-min grace) |
  MISSING (after cutoff) | NA (not yet due → never a finding).
- **Severity maps** (`severity.py`, the single reviewable place): security
  CRITICAL→CRITICAL, **WARNING→MEDIUM** (dynamic-IP login fatigue — genuine
  threats already emit CRITICAL), INFO→INFO; config_auditor BLOCK→CRITICAL,
  WARN→HIGH, INFO/PASS→INFO.
- **Health score** (`health.py`, OPEN findings only, ACK-suppressed):
  C=100 / H=50 / M=20 / L=5 / INFO=1 → ≤50 HEALTHY, ≤150 WARNING, ≤300 ATTENTION,
  else CRITICAL.
- **Disk thresholds** (`disk.py`): usage HIGH ≥85% / CRITICAL ≥95%; backup-growth
  ≥500MB, log-growth ≥300MB vs the prior trend row (history-gated).
- **Telegram (noise-controlled DELTA, `reporter.py`):** pushes ONLY a CRITICAL
  (unless ACKNOWLEDGED) or a NEW/reopened HIGH; MEDIUM/LOW/INFO never push; a clean
  run is SILENT (the heartbeat + `last_successful_run` prove it ran). Uses the REAL
  `TelegramNotifier.from_env`.
- **Pull report (never pushed):** `data_store/control_tower/report_<date>.{html,csv}`
  — findings (OPEN/ACK/RESOLVED) + freshness + 7-day trends + largest folders/files.
- **Operator ACK CLI** (`ops/control_tower/cli.py`): `ct list` (OPEN+ACK) ·
  `ct ack <id>` (suppress from the health score + the push) · `ct unack <id>`
  (reopen). Run `PYTHONPATH=. venv/bin/python ops/control_tower/cli.py list|ack|unack [id]`.
- **Tables (schema v40, pure addition):** `control_tower_findings` (dedup identity
  category/resource_name/reason; OPEN→ACKNOWLEDGED→RESOLVED) · `_runs` · `_trends`
  (per date: sizes + health) · `_status` (per run_date: per-domain statuses +
  overall band + `last_successful_run`) · `_freshness` (per stage).
- **daily_report observability:** `reports/daily_report.py` records
  `cron_heartbeat("daily_report")` on a successful xlsx write (Phase 1d source-fix
  for the 23-Jun false "daily_report missed" MEDIUM).
- Deferred T1/T2/T3 (NOT Phase 1): disk-metric repair, a retention job, weekly
  advisory scanners (the Sun-18:10 slot). Detail: memory `control_tower_phase1a_29jun`.

## Changelog
- 2026-07-28 — Claude Code (Opus 5) — **CAPITAL-DRIFT KILL LADDER — INVESTIGATION ONLY, ⛔ NO VALUE CHANGED (yaml/defaults/fixtures untouched; `git diff HEAD -- config/ capital/ core/ orders/ signals/ main.py` empty; thresholds still 250/1,000/2,500 and `consecutive_cycles_before_escalate: 3`).** ✅ **DECISION #11 IS CLOSED (Rama, 28-Jul 15:04): Option A — absolute, VALUES UNCHANGED (250/1,000/2,500, cycles 3), NO code change, NO boot slot.** An intermediate ₹1,500 soft-kill figure proposed earlier the same day was **WITHDRAWN, not deferred** (recorded, not deleted — a withdrawn figure that vanishes gets re-proposed), and the "earliest slot is after 4-Aug" line it implied is **struck**: there is no slot because there is no change. Reopen only on a material capital change, the ladder actually firing, or a drift distribution becoming obtainable. ⭐⭐ **THREE FINDINGS MADE UNDERNEATH IT SURVIVE THE CLOSURE and are registered as K1–K3 in `docs/decisions/ACTIONS_not_decisions.md`** — ⛔ a closed decision must not bury them. **K1: an EMERGENCY kill + a SAME-DAY restart ⇒ `StartupScenario.HALT`, exit 4, no boot without `--resume`, and in that window no exit management and no 15:15/EOD squareoff** — and it is a **CLASS, not a drift item** (26 non-test `soft_kill`/`hard_kill` call sites vs exactly **two** literals in `SCHEDULED_KILL_REASONS`, so every other path is an emergency kill). ⭐ **But it is MONITORED, which is the difference between a bounded and an open-ended hazard:** systemd neither retries nor loops (`RestartPreventExitStatus=3 4`, verified in `deploy/systemd/trading-system.service:35` **and** on the VM), and `liveness_probe` (cron `*/5 9-15 * * 1-5`, verified in the live crontab) raises **ONE CRITICAL within ~5 minutes** for a not-active unit during [09:00,16:00) on a trading day. ⛔ **Recovery is the weak link: `deploy/resume.sh` works but is named in NO incident document, and both docs that touch this are WRONG** — `docs/RUNBOOK.md:101` describes a *"Restart loop (exit code 4)"* symptom that **cannot occur** (the unit exits once and stays `inactive`) with a cause that was **fixed** (a stale/prior-day kill auto-clears since 20-Jun; the real cause is a SAME-DAY kill), and `docs/05_incident_response.md:40` says *"Manual resume: restart the service"*, **which is exactly what fails with exit 4**. A two-line doc correction with zero deploy consequence; deliberately NOT done in a register-only session. **K2:** the 06-Jul ₹10,000 events remain unresolved (real discrepancy vs seeding artefact — both answers matter). **K3:** the observability gap below. Decision file `docs/decisions/11_absolute_kill_ladder.md` → **CLOSED**. **WHAT THE CODE ACTUALLY DOES (read from source, not the config comments — it matched Rama's description on both rungs):** `capital/drift_handler.py::_compute_tier` buckets |delta| into NOISE/LOG_ONLY(≥250)/SOFT(≥1,000)/HARD(≥2,500); SOFT calls `kill_switch.soft_kill()` which **blocks new ENTRIES and genuinely ALLOWS EXITS** — enforcement is `is_active(intent)`, where `exit` is blocked **only** on HARD — and HARD calls `hard_kill()`, which blocks all orders **and dispatches an async FLATTEN** (cancels resting orders, exits `OPEN`/`PARTIAL`/`PENDING_FILL`, 2 h max retry, per-trade escalation alert), so **HARD does not abandon positions**; its `exit` block exists because the kill switch's own indestructible flatten owns the close. ⛔⛔ **THREE CORRECTIONS TO WIDELY-HELD ASSUMPTIONS.** (1) **ESCALATION IS SINGLE-SAMPLE** — `consecutive_cycles_before_escalate: 3` gates **only** the LOG_ONLY→SOFT_ESCALATED promotion; `TIER_SOFT`/`TIER_HARD` **reset** the counter and dispatch immediately, so ONE ≥₹1,000 sample soft-kills and ONE ≥₹2,500 sample hard-kills. (2) Its wall-clock is **≈45 s** via `fund_manager_self_check` (reconciler CHECK7, `poll_interval_sec: 15`, runs every cycle at `order_reconciler.py:1002`) and **UNREACHABLE** via `fund_manager` — `sync_from_broker` has exactly **ONE** production caller, `main.py:995`, a **one-shot at 09:15**, so that source fires once per process lifetime and the in-memory counter dies at the 17:35 self-exit. (3) The persisted-kill HALT is **bounded to a SAME-DAY restart**, not overnight: a drift reason is not in `SCHEDULED_KILL_REASONS` so it is an EMERGENCY kill and a same-day restart hits `StartupScenario.HALT` (**exit 4**, no exit management, no 15:15/EOD squareoff until `--resume`) — but `clear_stale_state()` is a **HEADLESS GUARANTEE** (Rama, 20-Jun) clearing **every** prior-day kill regardless of type. ⭐⭐ **MEASURED READ-ONLY, WITH THE SEARCH WIDTH PROVEN FIRST (22 files `system_2026-06-29`→`2026-07-28`, 1,539,561 lines, no `.gz`/archive = the whole on-disk history): THE LADDER HAS NEVER FIRED.** Total `drift_handler` lines = **2**; escalating-source events = **ZERO**; no log_only/soft/hard trip; no kill `triggered_by=drift_handler`; `HARD_KILL ACTIVATED` = 0 (the 19 `SOFT_KILL ACTIVATED` are the scheduled 15:15/EOD kills). **The only drift ever observed at hard magnitude came from a source the ladder deliberately ignores** — 06-Jul ×2, `source=order_reconciler`, tier=HARD, **delta ₹10,000** (4× HARD, 200× the ₹50 reconciler tolerance), logged at **INFO** by design because `order_reconciler` is not in `_ESCALATING_SOURCES`; ⚠️ its `expected=0.00` is the "~zero expected, possible publisher bug" shape and looks like a **seeding artefact**, so it must not be cited as evidence of real ₹10k drift. ⛔ **AND THE LIMIT, STATED NOT GLOSSED: a realised-drift DISTRIBUTION IS NOT OBTAINABLE from these logs** — `TIER_NOISE` logs at **DEBUG** and production runs at INFO, so every drift below ₹250 is invisible; min/median/p95 need a code change (raise the noise tier or persist samples) plus time. `kill_switch_state` is a **single-row CURRENT-STATE table, not a history**, so it cannot answer "has soft ever tripped" either. ⇒ decision #11's original "cheap and unblocked" framing was **wrong**: the *trip* question was cheap, the *distribution* question is not, and any value chosen now is chosen against an unmeasured background on a path that has never fired. ⭐ Both halves of this were found by applying the standing rule — **an absence is only established by a check wide enough to have found the thing**: a grep for `"capital drift detected"` returned 0 while `"tier":"HARD"` returned 2, because the JSON key is `msg`, not `message`.
- 2026-07-28 — Claude Code (Opus 5) — **STRAY-`.pyc` DETECTOR — `<BUILT>`, LOCAL/UNCOMMITTED, NOT PUSHED (Thursday 30-Jul gate). MONITORING-ONLY: it can never change trading behaviour.** An **11th** isolated check in the existing `scripts/system_manager.py` (`stray_pyc_check`, 18:45 Mon-Fri EOD job) — the check count has run 8 (19-Jun) → 10 (security + slippage) → **11**. ⛔ **No new file, no new cron, no new report path**; the EOD report gains one section. **THE PROPERTY, MEASURED NOT ASSUMED (PEP 3147):** `pkg/__pycache__/x.pyc` with no `x.py` is **NOT importable** (a cache keyed to an existing source) — Type A, a search nuisance; `pkg/x.pyc` with no `x.py` **IS importable** (the legacy sourceless layout) — Type B, the real hazard. So the trigger is not "a `.pyc` exists" but "a `.pyc` sits where a `.py` would". **WHY IT EXISTS:** `post-receive` deploys with `git checkout -f`, which overwrites tracked files but **never removes untracked ones**, and all bytecode is untracked — production proves the residue survives: `tests/unit/test_daily_review.py` was deleted in `01e07b7` yet its bytecode has sat in the deployed tree for 2+ months (inert, Type A — but the same mechanism would preserve an importable Type B indefinitely). **SEVERITY IS CONDITIONAL ON THE PROPERTY, NOT THE OCCURRENCE COUNT** (a "warn once, escalate on the second" ladder would let the dangerous case sit at WARNING for a whole night): sourceless ⇒ `violation()` ⇒ report goes CRITICAL; beside its `.py` ⇒ `warn()` (inert residue). ⛔ **Never sets `soft_kill_reason`** — a stray build artefact must not be able to stop tomorrow's trading; asserted by test, not merely intended. **Exclusions are config** (`_PYC_SCAN_EXCLUDED_DIRS = ("venv","sats")`) with a comment saying what an entry means; ⛔ nothing added speculatively — measured the same day, there are **ZERO `.pyc` outside `__pycache__` anywhere** (PC tree, deployed tree, and inside both venvs), so the entries exist for scan cost/ownership, never to silence a known false positive. ⭐⭐ **PROVEN-TO-BITE (the point of the item — its trigger has measured zero occurrences, exactly the "configured but never covered" shape this project keeps getting caught by):** 6 tests appended to the **existing** `tests/unit/test_system_manager.py`, every plant inside `tmp_path` so nothing is ever written into the repo and **nothing is ever planted in the deployed tree** — sourceless ⇒ CRITICAL · beside-source ⇒ WARNING not CRITICAL · excluded path ⇒ SILENT · Type A orphan ⇒ SILENT · clean tree ⇒ SILENT · the real repo currently clean. **3 code plants all went RED on exactly their own branch** (severity downgrade · `__pycache__` filter dropped → 2,259 violations, which also proved the real-repo test non-vacuous · exclusions emptied); restore verified **byte-identical by md5 + length**. ⛔⛔ **`git clean -xdf` MUST NEVER BE RUN IN THE DEPLOYED TREE** — `data_store/` (live DB **and** its backups), `logs/` and `.env` are all untracked or ignored there; the prohibition is written in the **code comment**, where somebody reaching for the quick fix is standing, not only in a report. ⛔ **CORRECTION carried into the docstring:** `.gitignore:46` is `*.py[cod]`, so a Type B is **ignored** and `git status` shows nothing — **git is blind to this class on the PC as well as the VM** (verified via `git check-ignore`; the earlier "no bare `*.pyc` rule, so the PC would see it" claim was false, now fixed in `docs/audit/pyc_orphan_sweep_28jul2026.md` §4). ⇒ **this check is the only detector on either machine.** **Regression — GATE PASSED, both runs MEASURED:** BASE `b5c11ae` **13F/5356P/4skip** (13:53→14:08) vs AFTER **13F/5362P/4skip** (14:09→14:23) — **`comm` EMPTY BOTH WAYS** (no new failure, none silently fixed), **+6 = exactly the 6 new tests** (collection confirms 6 `stray_pyc` node IDs). **Predicted before the run and met exactly.** ⭐ A fresh BASE was taken in the same session rather than trusting a remembered count — and in the **same time window**, so neither run crosses the 18:15 startup-test flip that changes the known-failure set; (3) `order_reconciler._recover_in_flight_entries()` runs as a **PREPASS at the top of every reconcile cycle** (startup synchronous `reconcile_once()` + the 15-min poll) — BEFORE CHECK1 and the G5b loop — so an adopted-OPEN trade gets its recovery SL (G5b) + TGT retry **in the SAME cycle**, never naked for a poll interval. BOTH feeds (timeout `UNKNOWN_IN_FLIGHT` queue + crash orphaned-`PENDING`) flow through ONE `_adopt_or_fail`: **terminal+filled→ADOPT** (backfill fill fields + ENTRY row COMPLETE, set OPEN, commit capital, flag TGT retry — even a partial-then-CANCEL is protected, never disowned); **terminal+zero-fill→FAILED+release** (the ONLY evidence-based release); **non-terminal (resting)→DEFER** (reserve retained, re-correlated next cycle); **ABSENT→FAILED+release only after a 3-poll propagation budget** (broker-reachable); **broker unreachable→DEFER the whole set** (never blind FAILED); **AMBIGUOUS→CRITICAL alert + defer** (never mis-adopt/release); **HARD_KILL + matched fill→FLATTEN** (commit→EXITING→oversell-guarded emergency close, finalized by `_check_stuck_exiting`→CHECK1; never resumed). (4) `broker/order_monitor._cleanup_orphaned_pending_trades` is now **DETECT-ONLY** (WARNING log; NO blind FAILED / NO orphan-callback release) — it defers to the reconciler recovery (which runs first at startup). **CAPITAL (crash-aware, exactly-once):** new `fund_manager.commit_adopted_entry` / `restore_adopted_reservation` / `release_adopted_reservation` — a crashed entry's reservation (rehydrate replays OPEN/PARTIAL only, so it's absent) is reconstructed in-memory from the durable `fm_ledger` RESERVE row, then committed/released; the three-balance invariant holds and the ledger stays consistent (RESERVE→COMMIT, or RESERVE→RELEASE, no duplicate/double). `state_store.adopt_recovery_trade_to_open` extended to backfill the fill fields atomically + new `mark_recovery_trade_exiting`. **RAMCOIND L1-L4 / CHECK9 (FACET-1 race-aware + FACET-2 oversell) / G5b all PRESERVED** (adoption reuses the existing guarded exit protocol; entry_time backfilled to `created_at` so G5b's settling window doesn't defer the recovery SL). **NO schema change.** Old `_check_unknown_in_flight` (timeout-only, blind FAILED after 3 polls, keyed on a broker_order_id a timed-out entry never learned) is REMOVED. Files: `broker/zerodha_adapter.py`, `orders/order_reconciler.py`, `broker/order_monitor.py`, `capital/fund_manager.py`, `core/state_store.py`. Tests: NEW `tests/unit/test_a1e1_recovery_matrix.py` (7 capital-correctness-proof cases + 13-row recovery matrix incl. same-cycle-G5b integration + paper-parity), `tests/unit/test_a1e1_orphan_recovery.py` (B.1 units), `tests/unit/test_fix068_timeout_recovery.py` retargeted (order-placer side kept; obsolete reconciler-method tests removed), 2 `test_order_monitor` FIX-071-Part-B tests updated to the detect-and-defer contract. Affected-suite regression green. Design: `docs/design/a1_e1_orphan_fix_design_02jul2026.md`; memory `a1_e1_orphan_fix_impl_02jul`.

- 2026-06-26 — Claude Code (Opus) — **FIX-183: orphan-GTT ADOPTION / reconstruction — closes the C2.1 gap (a live broker GTT with no `gtt_state` row was never reconstructed; no schema; delivery_enabled stays false).**
  Completes the locked SLICE2.5-P2 principle "**broker GTT = authority, `gtt_state` = self-healing mirror**": a LIVE broker GTT (status `active`) with NO `gtt_state` row is now ADOPTED instead of left orphaned. **The gap (C2.1):** a carried CNC delivery holding lives in `holdings()`, NOT `positions()`, so the reconciler's `bp is None` → without an ACTIVE `gtt_state` row to exclude it, CHECK1 would mis-mark the trade `CLOSED_MANUAL` (and G5b/CHECK9 place a spurious SL). A row-less GTT (persist-failure at placement, or a GTT predating the table) therefore had no self-healing path. **STEP 1 — `cnc_gtt_monitor.adopt_orphan_gtts()`:** for each broker GTT — skip unless `status=='active'`; skip if a `gtt_state` row already exists for its `gtt_id` (`get_gtt_state_by_id`); else **RECONSTRUCT** every column except `trade_id` from the broker GTT (`_reconstruct_from_broker_gtt`: symbol/exit_side/qty from the legs, **SL/TGT split by trigger MAGNITUDE** not array index → robust to broker leg reordering; unparseable/empty-legs → WARN, leave alone) and **CORRELATE** `trade_id` product-aware (`_delivery_open_trades_by_symbol` via `get_all_open_trades`, which carries the ENTRY-leg product through its LEFT JOIN — bare `get_trades_by_status_and_symbol` lacks product). Outcomes: **exactly 1** open CNC trade with no existing ACTIVE row → ADOPT (`insert_gtt_state`, reusing the durable write path); **0** trades → can't adopt (FK forbids; leave the live GTT ALONE) + WARN; **>1** (only reachable via manual entry — `DUPLICATE_SYMBOL` blocks it otherwise) → WARN, no adopt; **1 but already has an ACTIVE row** → WARN, no adopt (a 2nd ACTIVE row would trip M2). **Adoption ONLY ever INSERTs a row or WARNs — it NEVER deletes a live GTT.** Unadoptable WARNs are de-duped **once per gtt_id per process** (`_adopt_warned`, mirrors FIX-182's once-per-symbol discipline) so the every-cycle prepass can't spam (esp. benign human GTTs that match the 0-trades case); alerts carry `source_module="cnc_gtt_adoption"`. **STEP 2 — placement (the timing fix):** a NARROW prepass `order_reconciler._run_gtt_adoption_prepass()` runs at the **top of `_reconcile()`**, BEFORE the delivery-exclusion build + CHECK1, fail-safe (no-op when no monitor wired; never raises). Because CHECK1 lives inside `reconcile_once`, adopting at its top guarantees **adopt-before-CHECK1 in BOTH paths** (startup `reconcile_once()` at `start()`, and the 15-min poll's `reconcile_once()` which runs before the monitor's own `reconcile()`) — WITHOUT reordering `_maybe_run_cnc_monitor` or any MIS-path check (minimal regression). Cost: `get_gtts()` once per ~15s reconcile cycle (LIVE) — the "margins" rate bucket (burst 8, 8/s) makes this trivial. **Parity:** paper `get_gtts()` returns the identical dict shape → adoption runs end-to-end in paper. **No DB schema** (`gtt_state` already exists from P2 → no `.backup` step). Tests: `tests/unit/test_cnc_gtt_adoption_fix183.py` (12, paper injected-state) incl. **the C2.1 regression** (row-less carried GTT → reconcile_once → trade stays OPEN + gains an ACTIVE row, NOT CLOSED_MANUAL; plus a no-monitor contrast that DOES go CLOSED_MANUAL = proof of the gap) and **the MIS regression** (intraday trade through reconcile_once with the prepass present → CHECK1 unchanged, no `gtt_state` row created). Full PC unit suite **3837 passed / 12 skipped / 0 regressions**; touched suites (cnc_gtt × 4 + order_reconciler) 113 pass; integration smoke 11 pass. **delivery_enabled stays false** (durability only — one of the go-live gates before true, alongside T2). **DEPLOYED to main `af4b784` 26-Jun ~11:11 IST** (one-time authorized push+restart; standing Rama-owns rule RESTORED after): ff-merged `fix-183-gtt-adoption-26jun` onto main (also carried the pending P2 docs-log `75d4eca`); push → post-receive clean + crontab auto-installed (no hook errors). The 11:11 restart **self-exited 0/SUCCESS at the HOLIDAY guard** (26-Jun = Muharram, NSE closed → banner in 786ms, before StateStore) — like the P1/P2 after-hours boots; **real boot = Mon 29-Jun 08:15**. Verified independently broker-session-free **5/5 PASS**: FIX-183 symbols present, `load_all()` clean (Config Auditor 0 BLOCK), `delivery_enabled=False`, **live schema_version=36 + gtt_state (14 cols)** (P2 migration already applied). - 2026-06-24 — Claude Code (Opus) — **SL/TGT after-check is now circuit-clamp-aware (stops false-flagging a legitimately band-clamped exit).**
  Slice-1's after-check (`order_placer._verify_exits_placed`) flagged PACEDIGITK (Tue 23-Jun) `exits_verified=0` "TGT 216.15 != intended 220.41" — the **only `exits_verified=0` trade in the whole DB**. Mechanism: the fill-recalc TGT (220.41) sat above PACEDIGITK's upper circuit, so `place_exits`→`clamp_exit_into_band` legitimately clamped the placed TGT down to the band ceiling 216.15 and placed it there; the after-check then compared placed (216.15, clamped) vs intended (220.41, pre-clamp) → false mismatch (`_exit_price_mismatch` was even designed to catch clamps). **Fix (root cause, no schema):** the clamp ceiling was already in memory — `ExitLegsResult.tgt_price`/`.sl_trigger_price` hold the clamped price — so added `sl_clamped`/`tgt_clamped` bools to `ExitLegsResult` (defaults False, set in `LimitTripleProtocol.place_exits` from `sl_res`/`tgt_res.was_clamped`) and new `sl_clamp_price`/`tgt_clamp_price` params on `_verify_exits_placed`. New `_explained_by_clamp(placed, clamp_price)` = clamp recorded AND `placed ≈ band ceiling` (same tick tolerance): a price differing from intended is excused as a NOTE ("TGT/SL clamped to circuit band X (intended Y)") with `exits_verified=1` and **no alert** ONLY when fully explained by the band; a placed price matching NEITHER intended NOR band (or no clamp) still flags **CRITICAL(SL)/WARN(TGT)** — discriminator, NOT blind suppression (the band reference is the clamp's computed output, independent of the read-back order row). **Covers SL + TGT** (a LONG's SL can be clamped UP off the lower circuit → also was false-flaggable). - 2026-06-20 — Claude Code — **Slippage tolerance → %-of-SL-distance (replaces today's flat-Rs tiers).**
  Calibration investigation (`slippage_calibration_data_20jun`) found **SL is fixed (signal-based) and
  TGT recalcs from the fill (FIX-013)** ⇒ entry slippage directly inflates the risk budget. Rama's model:

  mode-selectable: **sl_fraction (DEFAULT)** `tolerance = min(SL_dist × max_slippage_fraction(0.22),

  A/B; **`hard_max_slippage_rs(₹10)`** absolute ceiling in every mode; `also_apply_pct_check`
  belt-and-suspenders; disabled → legacy flat %. `SL_dist = |signal_trigger − sl_price|` (both available
  in `place()`; cap-only if SL missing). New `_compute_slippage_tolerance` / `_slippage_decision`
  (order_placer, pure/tested); guard uses them; the calibration log now records

  `system_events` row (`event_type=KILL_AUTO_CLEARED`; details JSON: previous_state / reason /

  stopped in `_shutdown`) re-attempts the TGT on exponential backoff
  (**30/60/120/240/480s, give up after 5** → position stays SL-protected) via
  `OrderPlacer.retry_tgt_for_trade` → `FullEntryEngine.place_deferred_tgt_only` →
  `LimitTripleProtocol.place_tgt_only` (re-clamps to the CURRENT circuit band each try —
  Bug D; the band may relax). Guards: SL must still be standing (never place a naked
  TGT), no double-TGT (idempotent), never place an unprofitable TGT, skip while the kill
  switch is active or outside market hours. The placed TGT is registered in `_fill_map` +
  order_monitor so a fill triggers the software OCO (cancels the SL). Telegram INFO on
  success / WARNING on give-up. Config `tgt_retry:` (enabled/poll_interval_sec/max_attempts/
  backoff_base_sec; optional, defaults reproduce the schedule). State lives in the trades
  table → survives restart. 18 tests in `test_tgt_retry.py`; 454 affected-suite tests green

- 2026-06-19 — Claude Code — **Bug B: reservation-aware DAILY_TRADES cap** (extends
  FIX-185 to the daily limit). Audit found the prior "B already correct" was only
  half-true: FIX-181 fixed *what* counts (rejects excluded → retry) and FIX-190 added
  funnel metrics, but the `DAILY_TRADES` check was still a bare `count_trades_today`
  DB read with **no in-flight accounting** — the daily-cap twin of the position-cap
  TOCTOU race FIX-185 already closed. `approve()` reads `daily_count` inside
  `portfolio_lock`, but the candidate's `PENDING_FILL` trade row is inserted later by
  `order_placer.place()` **outside** the lock, so a burst all read the same pre-burst
  count and passed → overshoot (18-Jun 8-vs-5). Fix mirrors FIX-185 exactly:
  `effective_daily = max(daily_count, count_settled_trades_today() + count_live_reservations())`
  — `settled_today` = today's executed trades minus `PENDING_FILL`; `live_reservations`
  = the in-flight half (reserved-not-placed + `PENDING_FILL`); the two partition with
  no double-count (commit pops the reservation at fill), and `daily_count` (incl.
  `PENDING_FILL`) is the restart floor. `reserve()` runs inside the same lock, so the
  count is race-consistent. Rejections enter neither term → slot frees → next signal
  retries to reach max (the "5→3 undershoot" was never a counting bug — just no further
  signals). New `state_store.count_settled_trades_today()` + 6 unit tests (burst race,
  final-slot, rejection-frees-slot, restart floor, 18-Jun overshoot replay, settled-count
  partition). 346 tests green locally. Files: `capital/risk_engine.py`,
  `core/state_store.py`, `tests/unit/test_risk_engine.py`, `tests/unit/test_state_store.py`.
  Activates on next restart. (NB: `REJECTED` is NOT a valid `trades.status` — rejects are
  `FAILED`/`CANCELLED` or never get a trade row.)

  verifying the assumed "EOD self-exit" that it did **not** exist (runtime loop only waits on the

  so a service started in-window ran all night (harmless after P1-B, but not clean). Added an
  `eod-self-exit` daemon thread (`_start_eod_self_exit_thread` / `_eod_self_exit_due`): once past
  the **configured** `trading_hours.service_window_end` (**17:35 IST**; a hardcoded 16:00 until
  25-Jul-2026 — ⭐ that constant had **two roles** and was split into `SERVICE_START_CUTOFF`
  (latest the service may **START**, now 18:15 = the schema's exclusive bound, tied to the 18:15
  forward-shadow cron) and the configured **stop**; binding them that way makes a crash-restart
  between the two — refused with exit 0, never retried by `Restart=on-failure` — impossible for
  any legal config value) **and** flat (`StateStore.count_active_positions()==0` over
  OPEN/PARTIAL/PENDING_FILL) it sets the shutdown event → `main()` exits 0 → systemd won't restart →
  token-watcher's exit-0-today path skips the restart → clean overnight + clean 08:30 start. **Never**
  exits while a position is open (stays up to manage residual positions; count error → stays up).
